# ORACLE KBK: Frontier Mathematics Attack Ammunition (2026-07-18)

**Mission:** transfer the Oracle’s validated deterministic search, symmetry, formal-proof, adversarial-audit, and receipt techniques into the broadest practical frontier-math target portfolio possible.

**Inventory size:** 660 curated campaigns + 654 current Erdős open/falsifiable/verifiable/decidable targets + 117 parameterized problem factories. This is **1,431 explicit launch objects**, with the factories expanding into thousands of parameter instances.

> Scope honesty: no finite document can literally list every open problem in mathematics. This dossier is designed instead as an exhaustive **machine-attack arsenal**: the complete current attackable Erdős metadata snapshot, hundreds of cross-domain named campaigns, and reusable factories that systematically generate the smallest unknown cases. Every target must pass the Verify-Open gate immediately before launch.

---

## 0. Non-negotiable doctrine

- **Headline progress is broader than “solve a millennium problem.”** Valid wins include: a new exact value; first classification; improved upper/lower bound; new infinite construction; certified nonexistence; a materially extended verified range; a formalized missing lemma; a corrected false statement; or a rigorous negative result killing a major candidate mechanism.
- **Never prove the wrong predicate.** Formalization quality does not rescue statement mismatch. The source statement, quantifiers, exceptions, and implication chain must be tested before any long run.
- **Current status is volatile.** Re-check the source page, papers, preprints, and recent announcements on launch day. A stale “open” tag is not evidence of novelty.
- **Attack the first unknown parameter.** Exact finite frontiers produce the highest ratio of publishable progress to compute.
- **Exploit symmetry before scaling.** Do not pay for equivalent objects.
- **Proof objects beat solver claims.** Every witness, unsat result, numeric inequality, or classification needs an independent checker or proof-producing trace.
- **Negative results are assets.** A broad construction family killed at scale is worth preserving, formalizing, and using to redirect search.
- **Budget by calibrated novelty per compute.** Probe first; scale only after the cost model and certificate path survive hostile review.

## 1. What transfers directly from current Oracle work

| ID | Transferable blade | Use on new problems |
|---|---|---|
| A01 | Group-action orbit reduction | Canonicalize objects under automorphism, multiplicative, affine, permutation, switching, or relabeling actions before search. |
| A02 | Canonical augmentation / isomorph rejection | Generate one representative per isomorphism class; reject equivalent branches early using nauty/Traces, bliss, GAP, Sage, or custom canonical forms. |
| A03 | Finite-rung case decomposition | Turn an infinitary-looking statement into a dependency-respecting finite ladder of cases and discharge each rung independently. |
| A04 | Exact cover | Encode incidence requirements as exact-cover rows/columns; solve with Algorithm X/DLX or SAT while retaining a standalone verifier. |
| A05 | SAT / SMT / CP-SAT portfolio | Run multiple encodings and solvers; emit DRAT/LRAT/proof logs or independently check model certificates. |
| A06 | MILP / branch-and-cut | Use integer formulations, symmetry-breaking cuts, lazy constraints, and exact rational post-verification. |
| A07 | Constraint programming | Exploit all-different, cardinality, scheduling, and global constraints for construction and classification problems. |
| A08 | Backtracking with learned nogoods | Use domain-specific propagation, conflict learning, transposition tables, and deterministic branch ordering. |
| A09 | Meet-in-the-middle / subset transforms | Split exponential search, hash partial invariants, and use zeta/Möbius/Fourier transforms or bitset convolution. |
| A10 | Modular obstruction sieve | Search residue classes, local conditions, covering congruences, and CRT compositions before expensive global work. |
| A11 | Lattice reduction / integer relation | Use LLL/BKZ, closest-vector, PSLQ, and certified reconstruction to find or exclude Diophantine structure. |
| A12 | Interval arithmetic / exact numerics | Turn numerical evidence into proof using directed rounding, ball arithmetic, Taylor models, and branch-and-bound. |
| A13 | Generating functions / transfer matrices | Derive recurrences and exact counts from automata, state compression, rational series, or holonomic systems. |
| A14 | FFT/NTT and bit-parallel DP | Push exact finite ranges through convolution, bitsets, SIMD, compressed state, and external-memory checkpoints. |
| A15 | Flag algebra / SDP / SOS | Search for extremal inequalities numerically, rationalize certificates, and formally or independently verify them. |
| A16 | Spectral / semidefinite bounds | Exploit eigenvalues, association schemes, theta bodies, Delsarte LP/SDP, interlacing, and trace moments. |
| A17 | Polynomial method / Gröbner bases | Translate combinatorial or geometric constraints into polynomial ideals, Nullstellensatz certificates, resultants, or finite-field algebra. |
| A18 | Additive Fourier / character sums | Use group transforms, energy decompositions, density increment probes, and exact small-group experiments. |
| A19 | p-adic / local-global decomposition | Separate local solvability, Hensel lifting, valuation trees, and global obstruction search. |
| A20 | Automata / finite-state reduction | Recognize digit, carry, substitution, or recurrence structure and prove behavior through a finite transducer. |
| A21 | Rigorous graph census | Generate all objects in a bounded class, calculate invariants, and emit canonical hashes and independently checkable witnesses. |
| A22 | Counterexample miner | Negate the claim, optimize the smallest witness, diversify seeds/encodings, and adversarially validate every candidate. |
| A23 | Construction synthesizer | Search grammar-, algebra-, recurrence-, or template-defined families and infer reusable parametric constructions. |
| A24 | Theorem-gap extractor | Map a conjecture to the exact missing lemma, quantify hypotheses, and attack the weakest sufficient bridge rather than the headline directly. |
| A25 | Formalization-first search | Formalize the statement and dependencies early to detect vacuous predicates, hidden assumptions, and false reductions. |
| A26 | Checker + soundness theorem + sample witness | Keep the scalable witness data outside the kernel while proving once that the checker implies the mathematical statement. |
| A27 | Dual independent verifiers | Use methodologically distinct implementations and corruption tests; require byte-stable regeneration where possible. |
| A28 | Proof-producing solver integration | Capture LRAT/DRAT, VeriPB, Farkas, Gröbner, interval, or algebraic certificates and translate them into durable receipts. |
| A29 | Cost-model target ranking | Fit runtime and success models to completed attacks; select the next target by expected novelty per compute and uncertainty. |
| A30 | Bandit / portfolio orchestration | Probe several techniques cheaply, allocate budget adaptively, and retain failures as training data. |
| A31 | Import-graph / proof build optimization | Minimize formal-library dependency closure, shard compilation, and cap workers by measured peak RSS. |
| A32 | Parameter frontier scanner | Find the smallest unknown parameter or first unclassified case and attack that publishable boundary. |
| A33 | Literature-to-claim verifier | Resolve current status, nearest prior bound, exact definitions, and whether a candidate result is genuinely new before compute. |
| A34 | Sequence/OEIS conjecture miner | Reproduce terms, infer recurrences/asymptotics/congruences, test against held-out terms, then seek proof or counterexample. |
| A35 | Database anomaly miner | Scan LMFDB, House of Graphs, KnotInfo, code tables, and design databases for missing cases, outliers, and false metadata. |
| A36 | Adversarial statement audit | Try equivalent restatements, degenerate cases, quantifier flips, and known exceptions before accepting any proof target. |
| A37 | Negative-result certification | Prove a natural construction family, heuristic, or reduction cannot work; publish the killed lane and narrowed search space. |
| A38 | Human-readable proof extraction | Mine invariants and recurring clauses from solver traces, then compress them into lemmas or a parametric argument. |
| A39 | Reproducible long-run harness | Checkpoint, kill process trees safely, retain manifests, hash inputs/outputs, and resume deterministically across machines. |
| A40 | External adjudication packet | Bundle statement, provenance, code, receipts, independent checks, limits, and novelty comparison for expert review. |

### The highest-leverage transfers

1. **Multiplicative/group-action orbit reduction -> every symmetric finite classification problem.** Designs, Cayley graphs, codes, matrices, finite geometries, colorings, tilings, and graph generation should all be canonicalized before search.
2. **OmegaLadder finite-rung proof decomposition -> bounded classification theorems.** Decompose by prime-factor count, rank, degree, order, dimension, automorphism type, or local obstruction, then compile rungs independently.
3. **Steiner exact-cover/SAT engine -> all incidence structures.** Steiner systems, coverings, packings, Latin squares, orthogonal arrays, difference sets, graph decompositions, and tilings can share the same solver/checker spine.
4. **Checker + soundness theorem -> scalable formal trust.** One kernel theorem should establish that a small checker implies the target; millions of witnesses can then remain data, not bespoke proofs.
5. **JIGSAW -> cross-family target economics.** Runtime should be learned from density, propagation, symmetry quotient, proof size, and peak memory, not guessed from raw instance count.
6. **EPCE portfolio orchestration -> technique tournaments.** Cheap probes across SAT, CP-SAT, MILP, exact cover, specialized backtracking, SDP, algebra, and rigorous numerics should decide the production lane.
7. **Retraction-aware adversarial audit -> claim integrity.** Any changed premise or falsified bridge must automatically invalidate dependent routes and dossiers.

## 2. KBK Target Compiler: required pipeline

1. **Ingest:** source URL, exact statement, definitions, tags, prize, known finite data, formalization, and last status date.
2. **Verify open:** search current papers/preprints, source-maintainer updates, formal databases, and expert discussions.
3. **Normalize:** rewrite into explicit quantifiers, parameter domains, edge cases, and machine-checkable success conditions.
4. **Find the publishable frontier:** smallest unknown parameter, narrowest missing lemma, best known bound, or unclassified symmetry class.
5. **Detect structure:** automorphism group, product decomposition, local/global constraints, monotonicity, recurrence, spectral data, and known forbidden minors/configurations.
6. **Tournament techniques:** run small calibrated probes; rank expected novelty per wall-clock, RAM, proof size, and failure value.
7. **Production search:** checkpointed, deterministic, resource-capped, process-tree safe, and resumable.
8. **Independent verification:** different codebase/algorithm/language, adversarial corruption tests, and reproducible regeneration.
9. **Formal trust:** checker soundness theorem or proof-producing solver import; declare all axioms and computational trust.
10. **Novelty adjudication:** compare against nearest prior result and state the exact delta, no inflation.
11. **Release packet:** theorem/claim, data, code, hashes, logs, verifier, limitations, conditionality, and expert-facing summary.

## 3. Score interpretation

- **S (94–100):** launch now. Existing code or a very sharp finite frontier gives a realistic headline result.
- **A (86–93):** high-value next wave. Strong technique fit with manageable research risk.
- **B (76–85):** solid campaign. Likely needs new engineering, substantial compute, or deeper theory extraction.
- **C (64–75):** attack through side gates, restricted classes, rigorous numerics, or database completion.
- **D (<64):** do not attack head-on; use only for theorem-gap extraction, bounded subcases, formalization, or falsification of proposed routes.

### Recommended campaign mix

- **40% finite exact/classification:** designs, Ramsey numbers, graph tables, code tables, matrices, finite groups.
- **20% current Oracle closure:** unfinished receipts, missing Lean rungs, unattempted primes, cross-family tooling.
- **15% construction discovery:** adaptive number-theory families, Heffter/Cayley structures, codes, packings.
- **10% counterexample mining:** falsifiable formal conjectures, graph/dynamics/database claims.
- **10% theorem-gap extraction/formalization:** isolate weak sufficient lemmas and prevent statement mismatch.
- **5% high-risk rigorous numerics:** PDE/dynamics/geometry scenarios with interval certificates.

## 7. Parameterized problem factories

These **117 factories** are more important than a static list: each one continuously scans a parameter table for the smallest unresolved, weakly certified, or newly feasible instance.

| ID | Domain | Family | Parameter axes | Objective | Methods | Publishable outputs | Initial seeds |

## 8. Cross-domain transfer map

| Existing capability | Transfer targets | Required adaptation |
|---|---|---|
| Multiplicative orbit reduction | Heffter arrays; cyclic designs; Cayley graphs; difference sets; codes; circulant matrices; finite-field configurations | Find the acting group; canonical representative; stabilizer-aware workload; prove orbit coverage. |
| Finite-rung OmegaLadder | prime-factor classifications; graph degree/order cases; code dimensions; group rank; knot crossing number; design automorphism classes | Choose a monotone complexity parameter; split into exhaustive rungs; compile/check independently. |
| Exact-cover/SAT | Steiner systems; graph decompositions; Latin squares; tilings; covering arrays; MOLS; Ramsey colorings | Create at least two encodings; use symmetry breaking; capture proof trace or exhaustive canonical leaves. |
| Checker + soundness | large witness tables; graph classifications; matrix records; code constructions; numerical certificates | Formalize checker once; hash witness corpus; corruption-test verifier. |
| JIGSAW cost model | all parameter tables | Learn from density, propagation, quotient size, proof bytes, and peak RSS; rank the next frontier. |
| Import-graph optimizer | all Lean campaigns | Compute actual dependency closure; remove dominating imports only when measured; schedule by peak RSS. |
| Adaptive construction search | Erdős #950; additive bases; codes; packings; difference sets; prime covers | Represent construction as a policy/grammar; optimize held-out parameters; seek a provable invariant. |
| Negative-result certification | failed templates in number theory, combinatorics, geometry, and dynamics | Define the full family precisely; prove exhaustive failure range or asymptotic obstruction. |
| Database anomaly miner | OEIS; LMFDB; House of Graphs; KnotInfo; code/design tables | Recompute independently; flag impossible metadata, unverified records, missing smallest cases, and outliers. |
| Statement firewall | all domains | Generate known examples/exceptions; compare source and formal predicates; prove implication to the true target. |

## 9. Headline-progress ladders by problem type

### Exact finite value
1. Reproduce all published values. 2. Identify first table gap. 3. Find construction/lower bound. 4. Find obstruction/upper bound. 5. Close exact value. 6. Classify all extremizers. 7. Extract a formula or infinite family.

### Existence/nonexistence
1. Verify necessary conditions. 2. Partition by automorphism type. 3. Find candidate. 4. Independently verify candidate. 5. For nonexistence, obtain proof trace or exhaustive canonical leaves. 6. Formalize checker. 7. Generalize to a parameter family.

### Infinite conjecture
1. Formalize exact statement. 2. Locate weakest sufficient lemma. 3. Prove bounded ranges/subclasses. 4. Kill natural false mechanisms. 5. Improve quantitative constants. 6. Produce conditional theorem with transparent premise. 7. Only then attempt full closure.

### Numerical/analytic conjecture
1. Reproduce literature numerics. 2. Use interval arithmetic. 3. Certify a specific candidate or exclusion. 4. Convert observed structure into symbolic inequalities. 5. Rationalize certificates. 6. Formalize the finite reduction.

### Database problem
1. Mirror metadata. 2. Recompute invariants independently. 3. Find gaps/anomalies/extremes. 4. Verify source status. 5. Produce correction or new record. 6. Build continuous regression tests.

## 10. Targets to avoid attacking head-on

- Riemann hypothesis, P vs NP, Navier–Stokes, Hodge, Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer, Yang–Mills mass gap, and other grand conjectures should **not** receive undirected brute-force campaigns.
- They are admissible only through precise side gates: certified finite verification; explicit special classes; missing lemma formalization; counterexample validation of a proposed route; database completion; rigorous numerical bounds; or proof-tool infrastructure.
- Any campaign whose only output is “more evidence” without a certified record, new bound, theorem, construction, exclusion, or killed mechanism receives low priority.
- Any target with uncertain open status, ambiguous definitions, no nearest-prior comparison, or no independent verification path is blocked before production compute.

## 11. Mandatory launch checklist

- [ ] Exact source statement saved verbatim with URL/date/hash.
- [ ] Current open status verified from at least two credible channels when possible.
- [ ] Nearest published/preprint result and exact delta recorded.
- [ ] Formal predicate tested on all known examples and exceptions.
- [ ] Problem classified: exact finite / existence / bound / construction / counterexample / analytic lemma / formalization.
- [ ] Smallest publishable frontier parameter selected.
- [ ] Group actions and equivalences enumerated.
- [ ] At least two candidate encodings or algorithms probed.
- [ ] Cost model includes wall-clock, peak RSS, checkpoint size, and proof size.
- [ ] Success and stop conditions fixed before run.
- [ ] Independent verifier implemented before accepting success.
- [ ] Corruption tests demonstrate verifier rejects bad witnesses.
- [ ] Conditionality and trusted-computing base declared.
- [ ] All inputs, seeds, versions, commands, logs, and outputs hashed.
- [ ] Result regenerated from clean state.
- [ ] Novelty checked again after result, not only before.
- [ ] Adversarial review attempts to falsify statement, implication, code, and claimed delta.
- [ ] Release packet states limitations and incomplete pieces plainly.

---

## Final operating order

Do not choose the most famous problem. Choose the target with the strongest combination of **smallest unknown frontier, reusable Oracle blade, independent certificate, publishable delta, and calibrated feasibility**. Run multiple S/A-tier lanes in parallel, preserve every failure, and force each campaign to create either a theorem, a certificate, a construction, a classification, a corrected record, or a rigorous obstruction.
